###### **Chapter 11** 

#### **Exercise Collapse Associated with Sickle Cell Trait** 

##### **SICKLE CELL TRAIT** 

11-1. Sickle cell trait (SCT) is a genetic condition that occurs when a person inherits one sickle cell gene and one normal hemoglobin gene.  However, a person with SCT usually does not have any of the symptoms of sickle cell disease (SCD), which results from a hemoglobin gene mutation that affects the size and shape of red blood cells.  This mutation causes deformity of the red blood cells, which become sickle or crescent shaped, and affects the body’s ability to transport oxygen.  For more information on SCT and SCD refer to the National Institute of Health website. 

11-2. Service members with SCT can typically participate fully in any physical activity; however, under certain circumstances, Service members with SCT may suffer negative effects such as muscle weakness, pain, and collapse. 

11-3. This collapse is termed Exercise Collapse Associated with Sickle Cell Trait (ECAST).  Leaders will consider ECAST as a cause of collapse in Service members because the relative risk of death is significantly higher in Service members with SCT than those without.  However, early recognition and proper treatment of ECAST can increase survival and decrease complications.  For more information on ECAST refer to the American Medical Society for Sports Medicine website. 

##### **RISK FACTORS** 

11-4. The following is a list of preexisting medical conditions and environmental factors that may increase the likelihood of an ECAST episode: 

- Poor hydration. 

- Exercise-induced asthma. 

- Dietary supplements containing stimulants. 

- Excessive heat exposure. 

- High altitude. 

- High humidity. 

- Inadequate rest between intense exercise periods (e.g., prolonged wind sprints). 

- Intense exercise for an extended period. 

- Pre-exercise fatigue due to illness. 

- Returning from prolonged leave after little physical activity. 

- Inadequate acclimatization to environment. 

##### **SIGNS AND SYMPTOMS** 

11-5. Service members experiencing ECAST may have the following signs and/or symptoms: 

- Increasing pain and weakness in muscles, especially the large muscles of the legs, buttock(s), and low back. 

- Muscle tenderness. 

- Slumping to the ground. 

- Rapid breathing. 

- Slightly elevated body temperature (usually less than 103 degrees Fahrenheit). 

**ATP 4-25.12 (INCL C2)** 

**30 April 2014** 

**11-1** 

**Chapter 11** 

11-6. Some of the differences between the signs and symptoms of ECAST and other causes of exercise — related collapse include 

- Service members with ECAST will have soft, weak muscles while Service members with muscle cramps will have firm, tight muscles. 

- Service members with cardiac issues experiencing an ECAST event may not be able to speak or think normally. 

- Service members with ECAST will have a slightly elevated body temperature while Service members experiencing exertional heat stroke will have a core body temperature above 104 degrees Fahrenheit. 

- Service members with ECAST will usually be able to speak and think normally while Service members suffering from heat illness may present as confused. 

##### **PREVENTIVE MEASURES** 

11-7. To prevent ECAST, early identification of SCT in Service members is needed.  This is done with a simple blood test.  Defense Health Agency (DHA)-Procedural Instruction (DHA-PI) 6025.14. _Active Duty Service Member (ADSM) Erythrocyte Glucose-6-Phosphate Dehydrogenase (G6PD) Deficiency and Sickle Cell Trait (SCT) Screening;_ Department of Defense Instruction 6465.01, _Erythrocyte Glucose-6-Phosphate Dehydrogenase Deficiency (G6PD) and Sickle Cell Trait Screening Programs_ ; and ALARACT 094/2020, _Implementation of Army Sickle Cell Trait (SCT) Screening of All Soldiers_ establish the Department of Defense’s, DHA’s, and the Army’s procedures to implement erythrocyte G6PD deficiency and SCT screening at— 

- Entry to the Military Services (at appointment, enlistment, or induction). 

- Pre-deployment. 

- Other points as indicated for validation of results. 

- Acclimate to the altitude or temperature at which training will occur. 

- Exercise for shorter time periods and with less intensity during hot and/or humid conditions. 

- Hydrate before, during, and after exercise. 

- Understand the signs and symptoms of ECAST.  If any symptoms appear, report immediately to Holistic Health and Fitness staff, leadership, and/or health care personnel. 

##### **COMMAND RESPONSIBILITIES** 

11-8. Commanders will ensure that— 

- Every Service member in their command has been screened for sickle cell trait. 

- Every Service member with a positive SCT screening is issued and wears a red medical warning tag and receives a formal counseling from a unit or medical treatment facility (MTF) health care provider to discuss SCT-positive result. 

- During the counseling, the Service member receives educational information that includes— 

   - Erythrocyte G6PD and SCT deficiency diagnosis, causes, complications, treatment, and strategies to mitigate symptoms. 

   - Materials and resources that enable the Service member to self-identify symptoms experienced during exercise and when to seek immediate medical attention. 

- All Service members without record of a SCT screening are referred to a lab for screening unless the Service member can provide documentation of a prior screening.  The date and results of the SCT screening will be annotated in the Medical Protection System (MEDPROS) and recorded in the Service member’s electronic medical record. 

- Unit or MTF health care providers train all unit medical personnel to recognize the signs of ECAST, conduct an initial evaluation, provide treatment, and determine whether to call for urgent medical evacuation or immediately transport the individual to the nearest MTF. 

**ATP 4-25.12 (INCL C2)** 

**11-2** 

**30 April 2014** 

**Exercise Collapse Associated with Sickle Cell Trait** 

- A discrete method of identifying SCT-positive Service members (similar to those methods used to identify Service members who have sustained cold and or heat related injuries) is developed and added to the unit tactical standard operating procedure (TACSOP). 

- Unit or MTF health care providers will train unit medical personnel and leaders on actions to take during an ECAST event. 

**ATP 4-25.12 (INCL C2)** 

**30 April 2014** 

**11-3**
